        WARNING
     HIGH VOLTAGE

      is used in the operation
          of this equipment.

DEATH ON CONTACT

      may result if personnel
fail to observe safety precautions.

                                                                                                   vii


---

           DESTRUCTION NOTICE

      WHY — To prevent the enemy from using or salvaging this equipment.

      WHEN — When ordered by your commander.

      HOW  — 1. Smash—Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pickaxcs, hammers, crow-
                                 bars, heavy tools.

                 2. Cut—Use axes, handaxes, machetes.

                 3. Burn—Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame throwers, incendiary
                               grenades.

                4. Explosives—Use firearms, grenades, TNT.

                 5. Disposal—Bury in slit trenches, fox holes, other holes. Throw in
                                     streams. Scatter.

           USE ANYTHING IMMEDIATELY AVAILABLE FOR
                  DESTRUCTION OF THIS EQUIPMENT

      WHAT — 1. Smash—Chassis, panel, drum, lead screw, clutch, motor, tubes,
                                            transformers, fork oscillator, terminal strips, meters, con-
                                           trols, coupling units.

                        2. Cut—Cords, cables, wiring.

                          3. Burn—Covers, terminal boards, wiring, technical manuals, photo-
                                        graphic equipment.

                          4. Bend—Chassis, panels, drum, lead screw, table.

                        5. Bury or scatter—All of the above materials after destroying.

           DESTROY EVERYTHING

SAFETY NOTICE

   Facsimile Set AN/TXC-1(*)  uses high voltages which may be dangerous
to life.

   Voltages as high as 1,000 volts, 1,800 cycles alternating current, may be devel-
oped in the motor amplifier circuit of Facsimile Transceiver TT-1(*)/TXC-1.

   Voltages as high as 1,250 volts, 60 cycles alternating current, and 450 volts direct
current are developed in Rectifier Power Unit PP-86(*)/TXC-1.

   High voltage in the motor circuit of Facsimile Transceivers TT-1/TXC-1 and
TT-1A/TXC-1 is exposed when the cover is removed from the motor and clutch
assembly.

   Keep one hand in your pocket while making high-voltage measurements. This
precaution will prevent touching the electrical circuit with more than one part of
the body at one time.

   c. SyncHronous Motor. Motors on all three
equipments are mechanically interchangeable, but

motor parts are not interchangeable. All are syn-
chronous motors, controlled by a 1,800-cycle signal

generated in the fork oscillator unit and increased

in power by the motor amplifier tubes. Facsimile

Sets AN/TXC-1 and -1A use motors which are

started with 9-volt, 60-cycle/ac applied when the
START button on the transceiver is pressed.

Brushes are mechanically engaged to the start motor
during the starting operation. Facsimile Set AN/
TXC-1B uses an induction starting motor which
operates on 110-volt 60-cycle ac (fig. 13). No

9. Uncrating, Unpacking and Checking

Take care when unpacking or handling the equip-

ment. It may be damaged easily when it is not

protected by the packing case. When unpacking, be

careful to avoid damaging the packaging materials

any more than is absolutely necessary. Store the

inside packaging materials in the shipping con-

tainer for future use. Unpack and check the equip-

ment as follows:

    a. Cut the steel straps with a suitable cutting

device or twist with pliers until the straps crystal-

lize and break.

    b. Remove the nails with a nail puller and lift

off the top of the shipping container. Avoid prying

with a crowbar or other tool.

    c. Lift the packaged equipment from the box

and carefully remove the fiberboard box, the moist-                                                                                                                                               TL736861S

                                                            b. PERMANENT INSTALLATION. For a permanent
                                                         installation, locate the talking circuit in the wiring
                                                         coming into the telephone subset. This is usually

                                                         the two binding posts marked LINE on the

                                                         loss may furnish a received signal strength higher
                                                         than the LINE binding post input of the trans-
                                                         ceiver can accept. This is indicated by the necessity

     (Direct Process)                                    nations. If the drum stops, repeat the starting
                                                         operation.
  Direct process recording when     done on Teledeltos
  paper does not require reception  in a dark room and      (2) The transmitting operator will send approx-
  provides a single received copy   which can be used    imately 25 phasing pulses. These are indicated at
  immediately without processing.                        the receiver by a momentary dip on the db meter
_@, PREPARATIONS. (1) Wrap          the direct process   which occurs once for each revolution of the drum.
                                                         Be sure to wait for 10 to 15 pulses during which
                                                         the transmitting machine comes into proper phase.

24


---

Then press the PHASE button and hold it engaged
for about 6-10 pulses. The phasing pulses actually

bring the stop arm on the clutch mechanism into
phase, and from 5 to 10 more revolutions of the
stop arm may be required before the drum itself
falls into phase. The drum is not phased until the
stop arm is driving the dog trip arm (figs. 25, 26,
and 27). Check for this condition by looking at
the clutch mechanism through the window on the
clutch housing. If the drum is not being driven by the
stop arm driving the dog trip arm after 10 revolu-
tions following the release of the PHASE button,
exert a gentle friction with the fingers against the
phasing ring on the drum until the stop arm catches
up with the dog trip arm. (Facsimile Transceiver
TT-1B/TXC-1 has a drum friction mechanism
which makes this step unnecessary.)

   Note, lf the receiving operator misses the phasing pulses,
and is on a circuit where he cannot break the transmitting

                                                          ¥UU/

                                                                operator to ask for more phasing pulses, it may be possible
                                                                to phase the receiving machine as follows. Watch the db
                                                                meter for the periodic increase or decrease in reading once
                                                                each second. These abrupt -changes represent pulses sent
                                                                when the transmitting machine scans the clamp bar. Hold
                                                                the PHASE button down for a few pulses, When the re-
                                                                ceiving db meter flicks just as the clamp bar passes the
                                                                stylus or recorder lamp barrel, release the PHASE button.
                                                                There is a good chance that the receiving transceiver will
                                                                be properly phased and a suitable picture will be recorded.

                                                                the feed engaging lever forward, turn the selector
                                                                switch to RECORD DIRECT to engage the stylus,

                                                        33. Receiving Copy on Timefax Paper

                                                        Timefax paper is a specially prepared paper which
                                                        is used for reception when duplicate copies are
                                                        required. A hectograph pad is used for duplicating.
                                                        Timefax paper has a high-resistance outer coating
                                                        which is burned away by current from the trans-
                                                        ceiver stylus point when copy is received. The dark
                                                        undercoating of the paper is conductive. Recording
                                                        on Timefax paper is quicker and cheaper than the

   a. GENERAL. Follow the lubrication chart below                              TTT KCLE
in applying lubricants after the cleaning outlined

   b. Time IntERvAts. The lubrication chart is
based on 8-hour normal use daily. When the equip-
ment is in use more than 8 hours daily or is used
in tropical climates, it should be lubricated more
often. Items 4 through 12 especially should be
lubricated more frequently under conditions of
increased use or adverse climate. For example, if
the equipment is in use 16 to 20 hours daily, the
interval should be cut in half. In tropical climates
the lubrication periods can best be determined by
frequent inspection of all lubrication points. In
tropical climates the interval between lubrication
periods should be half or less than in temperate
climates.

                                                       77. Dustproofing

                                                       Signal Corps equipment operated in desert localities
                                                       is affected by the extremely high temperatures and

                                                       the amount of dirt, dust, sand, and other foreign
                                                       matter in the air. Take care to keep such elements
                                                       from filtering into lubricated parts. Cover the equip-
                                                       ment when it is not in use. Thorough cleanliness
                                                       is imperative. Instead of merely adding new lubri-
                                                       cants at regular intervals, whenever practicable,
                                                       clean and lubricate all moving parts. If possible,
                                                       inspect and clean the equipment daily. In any case,
                                                       inspect the air filters and similar protective devices
                                                       every day and clean them whenever necessary.
                                                       Refer to TB SIG 75, Desert Maintenance of Ground
                                                       Signal Equipment. Some of the problems encount-
                                                       ered are the following:

                                                          a. Lubricants become thin and drain from moving
                                                       metal and fiber parts rapidly. Refer to section VII
                                                       for detailed lubrication instructions.

                                                          b. Foreign matter, such as dirt, dust, and sand,
                                                       acts as an abrasive causing excessive wear, clogging
                                                       air cleaners, and impeding the flow of air.

                                                                                                                           89. Synchronous Motor
                             JAN —I635
                                                                                                                           The synchronous motor in Facsimile Transceiver
                                                                                                               TO START    TT-1(*)/TXC-1 is basically a Lacour phonic wheel
                                                                                                                 MOTOR     motor; its speed is held constant at 1,800 rpm by
                                                                                                                           the 1,800-cycle power supplied by the plate circuit
                                                                                                               TO 1I5V     of tubes V5 and V6. The worm on the motor shaft
                                                                                                               60 CPS AC   is meshed with a gear which drives the lead screw
                                                                                                                          at 60 rpm through the clutch mechanism.

98. General Trouble-Shooting Information                                              TL 10104:

faults occur, the repairman must locate and correct   CLZZ

general information to aid personnel engaged in       Figure 83. Use of tube lifter,

the material supplied in this technical manual. Con-

matic diagrams, wiring diagrams, signal tracing
charts, voltage and resistance charts, and the draw-
ings and photographs showing location of parts.

 102. Voltage and Resistance Measurements                        line voltage. For resistance tests, disconnect the
                                                                 power cord from the a-c line and remove the tubes.
 Follow the voltage and resistance charts (figs. 84              Make all tests from the bottom of the chassis.
 through 94) when localizing trouble. Make readings
 with the meter connected between chassis and the                   a. Only the following circuits are affected by
 indicated socket lug or component lug. Make all                 higher or lower line voltages within normal limits:
 voltage tests with the tubes in place, the power                filaments; unregulated B+; V14 stage when selector
 ON-OFF switch at the ON position and, unless                    switch is at RECORD DIRECT; regulator stages
 otherwise noted on the chart, with an input signal              V7, V8, V9, and V21; start motor; start coil; and
 sufficient to cause a reading of +2 on the db meter.            motor amplifier stages V4, V5, and V6.
 For d-c voltage measurements use a 1,000 ohms per
 voltmeter and for a-c signal measurements use a                    b. The motor amplifier is somewhat self-regulating
 vacuum-tube voltmeter, unless otherwise noted. Line             so that the motor current varies little over a wide
 voltage should be 115 volts; if it is higher or lower,          range of line voltages. The effect of the line voltage
make allowances in measuring voltages affected by                on this circuit is complex; in some cases the motor
                                                                 current or voltage increases as the line voltage
                                                                 decreases.

   Warning: Never run the motor with the clamp
screw G loose, or gears may be stripped.

  b. Seconpary CLutcu. The secondary clutch

tension should be approximately half that of the
primary clutch (6 to 7 ounces). If the secondary
clutch is too loose, the stop arm and dog strip will
come together too rapidly and possibly throw the
motor out of synchronism or slip the primary clutch.
Slipping of the primary clutch during the phasing
operation will result in out-of-phase recording. If
the secondary clutch tension is too heavy, the second-
ary clutch load added to the primary clutch load
may cause the motor to stall during the phasing
operation. Make a static measurement of the second-
ary clutch tension. With’the motor stopped, and
the string wrapped around the drum, pull the string
